In addition, we shall focus on the case of infinite Péclet number and of vanishing particle swimming velocity,

\operatorname{Pe}=\infty,\qquad U_{0}=0,

which substantially simplifies the macroscopic fluid equations (recall however that our rigorous results do not hold for
\operatorname{Pe}=\infty
).
We start by computing the contribution of the elastic stress
\sigma_{1}
in (
C.1
).
Using that

n\otimes n-\tfrac{1}{d}\operatorname{Id}=-\tfrac{1}{2d}\Delta_{n}(n\otimes n-\tfrac{1}{d}\operatorname{Id}),

we have by definition

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}[g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{3}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\theta\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(n\otimes n-\tfrac{1}{d}\operatorname{Id})\,(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{3})(\cdot,n)\,\mathrm{d}n

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{2d}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(n\otimes n-\tfrac{1}{d}\operatorname{Id})\,\Delta_{n}(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{3})(\cdot,n)\,\mathrm{d}n.

Using the defining equations for
g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}
with
U_{0}=0
, in form of

\Delta_{n}(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{3})=\tfrac{1}{\omega_{d}}{\operatorname{div}}_{n}(\pi_{n}^{\bot}(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})n)+\varepsilon(\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2})\\
+\varepsilon{\operatorname{div}}_{n}(\pi_{n}^{\bot}(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})n(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}))+O(\varepsilon^{3}),

the above becomes, after straightforward simplifications and integrations by parts,

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}[g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{3}]_{ij}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2\omega_{d}}\lambda\theta\operatorname{D}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}):\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(n\otimes n)(n\otimes n-\tfrac{1}{d}\operatorname{Id})_{ij}\,\mathrm{d}n

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{2d}(\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(n\otimes n-\tfrac{1}{d}\operatorname{Id})_{ij}(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2})(\cdot,n)\,\mathrm{d}n

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{2d}(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})_{kl}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(\delta_{ik}n_{j}n_{l}+\delta_{jk}n_{i}n_{l}-2n_{i}n_{j}n_{k}n_{l})\,(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2})(\cdot,n)\,\mathrm{d}n

\displaystyle\quad+O(\varepsilon^{3}).

Now inserting the explicit expressions for
g_{1},g_{2}
obtained in Proposition
3.1
, in form of

(C.2)

(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2})(\cdot,n)\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2\omega_{d}}(n\otimes n-\tfrac{1}{d}\operatorname{Id}):\Big(\operatorname{D}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\tfrac{1}{4d}A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon\tfrac{1}{d}\operatorname{D}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}\Big)\\
+\varepsilon\tfrac{1}{8\omega_{d}}\Big(\big((n\otimes n):\operatorname{D}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\big)^{2}-\tfrac{2}{d(d+2)}{\operatorname{tr}}(\operatorname{D}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2})\Big)+O(\varepsilon^{2}),

using the explicit integrals (
3.11
), and using that
{\operatorname{tr}}(A_{2}(u))=4{\operatorname{tr}}(\operatorname{D}(u)^{2})
,
we are led to

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}[g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{3}]

\displaystyle\qquad=\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{2d(d+2)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{d^{2}(d+2)(d+4)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\varepsilon\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{4d^{2}(d+2)}\Big((\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla)A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{T}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{8d^{3}(d+2)}\Big((\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla)A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{T}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{4d^{3}(d+4)}\Big((\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla)A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}-(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}-A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{T}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{4d^{3}(d+2)(d+4)}\Big(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{(5d+12)}{4d^{3}(d+2)(d+4)(d+6)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{(d+3)}{4d^{2}(d+2)^{2}(d+4)(d+6)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}){\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2})+R_{\varepsilon}\operatorname{Id}+O(\varepsilon^{3}),

for some scalar field
R_{\varepsilon}
that will be absorbed in the pressure field.
Now recalling the definition of Rivlin–Ericksen tensors, cf. (
4.7
), and appealing to the following matrix identities, for any matrix
B
,

\displaystyle-(\nabla u)B-B(\nabla u)^{T}\,=\,(\nabla u)^{T}B+B(\nabla u)-\big(A_{1}(u)B+BA_{1}(u)\big),

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)A_{1}(u)^{2}-(\nabla u)A_{1}(u)^{2}-A_{1}(u)^{2}(\nabla u)^{T}\,=\,A_{1}(u)A_{2}(u)+A_{2}(u)A_{1}(u)-3A_{1}(u)^{3},

we are led to

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}[g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{3}]

\displaystyle=\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{2d(d+2)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{4d^{2}(d+2)}A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{2d^{2}(d+4)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{8d^{3}(d+2)}A_{3}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{3}{8d^{3}(d+4)}\Big(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{(3d^{2}+19d+24)}{4d^{3}(d+2)(d+4)(d+6)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{3}-\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{(d+3)}{4d^{2}(d+2)^{2}(d+4)(d+6)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}){\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+R_{\varepsilon}\operatorname{Id}+O(\varepsilon^{3}).

Further recalling that
A_{1}(u)^{3}=\tfrac{1}{2}A_{1}(u){\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(u)^{2})+\tfrac{1}{3}{\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(u)^{3})
in dimension
d\leq 3
by the Cayley–Hamilton theorem with
{\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(u))=0
,
we obtain

(C.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sigma_{1}[g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{3}]\\
&=\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{2d(d+2)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{4d^{2}(d+2)}A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{2d^{2}(d+4)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}\\
&\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{1}{8d^{3}(d+2)}A_{3}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{3}{8d^{3}(d+4)}\Big(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\Big)\\
&\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\theta\tfrac{3d^{2}+11d+12}{8d^{3}(d+2)^{2}(d+6)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}){\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2})+R_{\varepsilon}\operatorname{Id}+O(\varepsilon^{3}),\end{split}

up to modifying the scalar field
R_{\varepsilon}
.

Next, we turn to the computation of the contribution of the viscous stress
\sigma_{2}
in (
C.1
). We have by definition

\sigma_{2}[\tfrac{1}{\omega_{d}}+\varepsilon g_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{2},\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}]\,=\,\lambda\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(n\otimes n)(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})(n\otimes n)\,(\tfrac{1}{\omega_{d}}+\varepsilon g_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{2})\,\mathrm{d}n,

and thus, using the explicit integrals (
3.11
),

\sigma_{2}[\tfrac{1}{\omega_{d}}+\varepsilon g_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{2},\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}]\\
\,=\,\lambda\tfrac{2}{d(d+2)}\operatorname{D}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon\lambda\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(n\otimes n)(\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})(n\otimes n)\,(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2})\,\mathrm{d}n+R_{\varepsilon}\operatorname{Id},

where again
R_{\varepsilon}
stands for some scalar field that can be absorbed in the pressure field.
Inserting the explicit expressions for
g_{1},g_{2}
obtained in Proposition
3.1
, in form of (
C.2
), and computing integrals as above, we easily find

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}[\tfrac{1}{\omega_{d}}+\varepsilon g_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{2},\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle=\lambda\tfrac{1}{d(d+2)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon\lambda\tfrac{1}{d(d+2)(d+4)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\tfrac{1}{4d^{2}(d+2)(d+4)}\big(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\tfrac{(5d+12)}{4d^{2}(d+2)(d+4)(d+6)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\tfrac{(d^{2}-2d-12)}{8d^{2}(d+2)^{2}(d+4)(d+6)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}){\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2})+O(\varepsilon^{3})+R_{\varepsilon}\operatorname{Id}.

Using again
A_{1}(u)^{3}=\tfrac{1}{2}A_{1}(u){\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(u)^{2})+\tfrac{1}{3}{\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(u)^{3})
in dimension
d\leq 3
, this becomes

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}[\tfrac{1}{\omega_{d}}+\varepsilon g_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}g_{2},\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle=\lambda\tfrac{1}{d(d+2)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon\lambda\tfrac{1}{d(d+2)(d+4)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\tfrac{1}{4d^{2}(d+2)(d+4)}\big(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})+A_{2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda\tfrac{(3d^{2}+10d+6)}{4d^{2}(d+2)^{2}(d+4)(d+6)}A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}){\operatorname{tr}}(A_{1}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})^{2})+R_{\varepsilon}\operatorname{Id}+O(\varepsilon^{3}).

Inserting this together with (
C.3
) back into (
C.1
), we precisely get the claimed third-order fluid equation (
4.34
).
∎
